門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

２％物価目標実現の「節目」に思う 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 2026 年 11 月 27 日

達成感なき目標実現 
日銀は 10 月 30 日、消費者物価の基調的な上昇率が２％に達したとの判断を示した。2％物価目標は
2013 年 1 月、デフレからの脱却に向けた政策の一環として導入され、13 年 9 か月を経て目標が実現し
た。その日の記者会見で植田総裁は「長い間そこを目指してきたという面では一つの節目」と述べたが、
日銀にも、この政策を後押ししてきた政府にも達成感や高揚感はない。理由はいくつかありそうだ。 
第一に、目標の実現まで想定よりはるかに長い時間がかかった。日銀は 2013 年 4 月、2％物価目標の
実現に向けた具体策として、量的・質的金融緩和（いわゆる異次元緩和）を発動した。その際「2 年程
度の期間を念頭に置いて、（2％物価目標を）できるだけ早期に実現する」と宣言した。実際には「2 年
程度」の 6 倍以上を要したのだから、1 回で合格を目指した試験に 6 回落ち、7 回目にやっと受かった
ようなものである（図表 1）。 
第二に、時間がかかった事実と関係するが、物価上
昇率が目標に達した理由も想定外だった。日銀が当
初 2 年程度の期間を念頭に置いたのは、一般に金融
政策から物価への波及にはその程度のタイムラグが
あるとされているからである。タイムラグは不確実
でもっと長い場合もありうるが、さすがに 13 年とい
うことはない。つまり、2％物価目標が実現したのは、
13 年前の金融政策とは無関係であり、予期せぬ外部
環境の変化が近年たまたま起きたからにすぎない。7
回目も自力で受かったわけではない。 
第三に、最も重要な点として、2％インフレになっても日本経済は改善しなかった。それは、今述べた
目標実現の理由と関係する。2％物価目標の実現をもたらした外部環境変化とは、コロナ禍後のサプラ
イチェーンの混乱、ロシア・ウクライナ戦争などを背景に生じた 2021～22 年の世界インフレである。そ
の後も 2025 年のコメ価格高騰、2026 年のイラン戦争など、インフレ・ショックが相次いだ。そうした
連続ショックが企業の賃金・価格設定行動を変え、2％ないしそれ以上のインフレが続くようになった。

異次元緩和

### Page 2

2

2013 年に異次元緩和を始めた時は、金融緩和や政府の施策で企業や家計のマインドが改善し、総需要
が増えて物価も上がっていくと想定されていた。あるいは、物価が上がるという予想が実質金利や資産
価格への影響を通じ、経済を刺激する効果を持つと考えられていた。 
しかし、実際に 2％インフレをもたらしたのは外
生的コスト・ショックであり、これは多くの国民に
とって「災難」でしかない。家計のマインドは改善
どころか悪化した（図表 2）。 直近の個人消費は 2 四
半期連続のマイナス成長であり、その水準はコロナ
禍前の 2019 年をも下回る。 
このように、2％物価目標が実現したと言っても、
①かかった時間、②実現のメカニズム、③経済への
影響のすべてが当初想定と全く異なる。「当初想定
した 2％物価目標」は結局実現しなかったのであ
り、達成感の湧きようがない。 
日銀が「基調的な物価上昇率」の判断に慎重だった理由 
10 月 30 日の植田総裁会見では「2％が持続的・安定的に実現したとはまだ言えず、今後は 2％程度の
水準で定着していくかどうかを見極めていく必要がある」と述べられたが、この説明には違和感がある。
前掲の図表 1 は消費者物価の「コア指標」として日銀が公表しているものであり、基調的な物価上昇率
を把握するうえで重要な指標とされている。その指標でみて 2％前後のインフレが約 4 年続いており、
さらに日銀の見通しでは 2028 年度まで 2％以上のインフレが続くとされているのだから、2％が定着し
ていないという日銀の説明は理解しにくい。 
一方、2％物価目標実現の中身が 13 年前の想定と全く異なる以上、「ミッション・コンプリート」と胸
を張れない日銀の心情は 100 パーセント理解できる。2％は定着していない、という実態に合わない説
明も、物価上昇の「質」を考慮したうえでの「未達成感」の表れなら、すとんと腹落ちする。そうした
未達成感こそ、「2％到達宣言」まで日銀が 4 年を費やした理由でもあったと思われる。 
基調的な物価上昇率は「一時的な変動を除いた物価上昇率」と定義されている。しかし、日銀の胸の
内では、その物価上昇率がどのくらい続いたかという期間だけでなく、良好な経済と表裏一体の物価上
昇なのか、つまり当初目指した性格の物価上昇なのかという質的な観点も、基調的な物価上昇率の判断
に影響したと考えられる。だとすれば、コストプッシュで消費者マインドが落ち込むような物価上昇は、
それが長く続いていても基調的な物価上昇とは呼びにくい。日銀にとって悩ましかったのは、そのよう
な物価上昇でもさすがに 4 年続くと、「一時的な変動を除いた物価上昇率」という外形的な定義に照ら
し、「基調的な物価上昇率はまだ低い」と言い続けられなくなったことだろう。中身には不満だが外形上
認めざるをえない、というのが「2％到達宣言」の実態だったのではないか。

２％インフレ局面

### Page 3

3

「デフレ脱却宣言」を出さない政府とも日銀の認識は同じ 
日銀が「2％到達宣言」を出したのに政府が「デフレ脱却宣言」を出さないのは、政府・日銀の認識に
ずれがあるからではないか、との見方がある。しかし、政府も物価上昇の質を重視しているのだと考え
られる。デフレというのは継続的な物価の下落のことであるが、過去においても実態としては、経済成
長率が低いという意味も込めてデフレという言葉は使われてきた。その延長で考えれば、物価が継続的
に上昇するようになっても、経済成長率が低いままの現状は、政府が心に描いていたデフレ脱却からほ
ど遠い。 
また、これは政府自身が決めたことだが、「再びデフレに戻らない」という将来の見込みも、デフレ脱
却宣言を出す条件になっている。物価上昇の長期化という事実に基づき「2％到達宣言」を出さざるをえ
なかった日銀と違い、将来の見込みという検証不能な要素で決まる「デフレ脱却宣言」は、政府がそれ
を出したくないなら永遠に出さなくてもよい。日銀の責務は「物価の安定」であり、政府は経済成長を
何とか高めたい、という立場の違いもある。逆に言えば、両者の違いはそれだけのことであり、思い描
いていた物価上昇が実現せず残念だという点では、政府と日銀の認識に違いはないだろう。                                                                                                                     
物価上昇で何か良いことは起きたのか 
その政府・日銀も、現状にはなお不満足ながら「低インフレやデフレのころに比べれば良くなった」
と捉えているようであるし、その種の議論は経済論壇でもよく見かける。しかし、それもそう言えるか
どうか定かではない。例えば、賃金の上昇について考えてみよう。近年は賃金も上がるようになって「賃
金と物価の好循環」が起き、賃金も物価も上がらなかった状態に比べて日本経済は良くなった、と言わ
れることが多い。しかし、二つ留意すべき点がある。 
第一に、賃金と物価の相対的な上昇率に注目すべきである。ここ数年の賃金上昇率は、物価の上昇率
に比べると平均的にみてやや低い。言い換えれば、インフレが始まってからの累積でみると、実質賃金
の水準は低くなっている。図表 2（前掲）のとおり人々が感じる「暮らし向き」が 2022 年以降厳しいま
まなのは、そのことが影響している面が大きいとみられる。 
第二に、賃金も物価も動くということ自体をどう考えるかである。それらがよく動く経済の方が、ゼ
ロ近傍で固まっている経済よりも、価格機能を通じて効率的な資源配分が促され、潜在成長率が上昇す
るとの見方がある。実際、日銀はその可能性に一時期言及していた。2024 年 3 月のマイナス金利解除が
近づいたころだったので、賃金や物価が上昇し金利を引き上げる状況になってきたことを、日銀はなる
べくポジティブに語りたかったのかもしれない。当時筆者は、その見方はおそらく間違いだと本欄で論
じた 1。その考えは今も変わっていない。実際、「賃金も物価も動く方が中長期の経済成長にプラス」と
いう主張は、これまでのところ現実のデータでサポートされていない。内閣府の推計による潜在成長率
は、デフレ脱却を目指して 2％物価目標を導入したころは 1％程度であったが、その 2％物価目標が実現
した今は 0.5％程度へ低下している。

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「インフレは生産性にプラス？ ―日銀「新理論」の問題提起―」（2024 年 1 月 24 日）。

### Page 4

4

インフレのもとでは企業収益や株価が上がりやすくなるという議論もある。企業収益や株価は名目値
なので、インフレで上がりやすくなるのは当たり前である。論点はそこではなく、それで企業や株主が
追加的に得をするのかである。インフレだと企業経営に必要なコストも上がる。株主も最終的には一消
費者であり、株のリターンのうちインフレで膨らんだ分は出費の増大で相殺される。先ほどの賃金と同
様、企業収益や株価もインフレ以上に改善（実質収益や実質株価が上昇）するかどうかが重要なのであ
る。実際のところ、近年の企業収益や株価は実質でみても大きく改善しているが、これは株主目線での
経営改革が進んだことによる面が大きく、物価変動とは関係なく起きた動きである。  
経済と物価の関係についてなお議論の深まりが必要 
そもそも 13 年前、日銀が 2％物価目標の実現に向けて異次元緩和を行った背景には、「金融緩和→物
価が上がる→経済が良くなる」という当時の経済論壇やメディアで一定の支持を集めていた考え方があ
った。しかし、前半の「金融緩和→物価が上がる」の因果関係は、出発点の金利がほぼゼロならあまり
働かないことがわかったし、後半の「物価が上がる→経済が良くなる」の因果関係も、少なくとも現状
では起きていないかむしろ逆のことが起きている。異次元緩和を始めた時の考えは間違いだらけだった
のであり、そのことがわかったという意味で人類の知見は少し増えた。 
それでも、物価が上がる経済の方がそうでない経済より望ましく、かつてはデフレだったから低成長
だったという認識が、依然として広く残っているように思う。かつてより今の方がもっと低成長である
にもかかわらず、である。また、賃金と物価が上がるようになったことで、賃金と物価の循環を巡る新
たな誤解も生まれている。例えば、賃上げの原資を確保するために企業は積極的に値上げすべきであり、
買う方も価格転嫁を受け入れるべきだ、という誤解である。企業に期待したい本当の経営努力は「なる
べく価格を上げずに賃金を上げること」であるはずだ。 
2％物価目標が実現されたのを機に、経済と物価に関する議論がさらに深まればよいと思う。

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）予測

～ほぼゼロ成長も、緩やかな景気回復は継続～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田 隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表する 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.0%（前期比年
率換算＋0.1%）と予測する。設備投資や政府消費の増加に支えられ、かろうじて 4 四半期連続のプラス成長を
維持したものの、個人消費や輸出が前期の一時的な押し上げ要因の剥落もあって減少したことで、ほぼゼロ成長
にとどまったと見込まれる。なお、輸入が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は大きめのマイナスと
なるものの、公的在庫のプラス寄与により、おおむね相殺されたと考えられる。 
また、名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.9%（年率換算＋3.8%）となったと見込まれる。コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進
む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比＋2.7%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.9%）と高めの伸びが続いた。 
今回、7～9 月期の実質 GDP はほぼゼロ成長にとどまる見込みだが、イラン情勢の悪化という逆風を踏まえれ
ば、国内景気は底堅さを維持している。先行きについては、輸入コストの高止まりに伴う価格転嫁の進展が景気
の重しとなる可能性はあるものの、良好な雇用・所得環境や企業業績の改善を背景に内需の回復基調が維持さ
れることなどから、国内景気は緩やかな回復を続けると期待される。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」により作成

### Page 2

2 / 2

需要項目別の動向は以下の通りである。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.1%） 
 良好な所得環境が維持される中、消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かったものの、前期に耐久財
が自動車やエアコンを中心に一時的な要因によって増加した反動が生じたとみられる。価格転
嫁の進展による物価上昇、地震や台風といった自然災害の発生も重しとなった可能性がある。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－0.3%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が横ばいとなる中、建築コストが上昇していることもあり、住
宅投資は小幅ながら減少が続いたとみられる。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋0.9%） 
 企業の投資意欲は省力化やデジタル需要などに支えられて堅調であり、設備投資は 3 四半期
ぶりに増加したと見込まれる。機械設備投資の供給側統計である資本財総供給（除く輸送機械）
も 7～9 月期は前期比＋5.3%と増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度－0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫などの積み増しを進めた反動もあ
り、7～9 月期はマイナス寄与になったと考えられる。 
 なお、公的在庫については、政府による代替調達の進展により石油備蓄の減少ペースが大きく鈍
化したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントとプラス寄与になったとみられる。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費支出（前期比＋0.3%） 
 高齢化が進む中、医療費・介護費を中心に底堅く推移したと予測する。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－2.7%） 
 建築・土木コストの上昇が続いている中、7～8 月の建設総合統計（出来高ベース：公共）は弱
めの動きとなったことから、実質公共投資は 2 四半期連続で減少したと見込まれる。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.5%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.5%、実質輸入：同＋1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.5%と減少したと予測する。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受
けて増加した一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動
で減少したとみられる。 
 輸入は前期比＋1.7%と増加したと予測する。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただ
し、サービスについては減少した可能性がある。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.5%ポイントとマイナスになったと見込ま
れる。

我们的观点：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大于指数整体波动。

1.  2026 年海外权益行情回顾：市场从极致 K 型分化走向结构性再平衡 
1.1   “AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情极致演绎：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性 
回顾2026 年，美股经历了“AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情的极致演绎，概括而言，美股指数韧性强于个股、
结构波动大于总量波动。上半年，地缘冲突压制宏观风险偏好，AI 产业景气构成基本面狭窄的繁荣，市场缩圈
至业绩确定性最强的AI 硬件资产。下半年，AI 下游商业化增长动能边际放缓叠加市场对CSP 资本开支可持续
性及投资回报的质疑，前期极致抱团的AI 行情逐步瓦解，拥挤的筹码结构放大了波动。与此同时，若排除地
缘扰动的外生影响，非AI 资产亦能展现较强的韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散，一定程度对冲了AI 板块调整的
负面影响，使得指数整体波动相对收敛。 
图 1：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性  图 2：上半年 AI 资产贡献标普 500 上涨主要驱动，下
半年非 AI 资产再平衡对冲科技下跌对于指数的拖累

1.2  行情核心驱动：AI 产业景气趋势主导方向，抱团交易瓦解放大波动 
2026 上半年：AI 产业景气突破成为核心驱动，盈利增长消化估值压力。上半年，尽管地缘冲突扰动持续
压制宏观风险偏好，但AI Agent（Anthropic）应用场景取得突破性进展进一步强化AI 产业稀缺的景气度，
较强的盈利增长对前期估值形成消化和支撑，驱动AI 板块上涨。

2026 下半年：AI 产业潜在风险逐步显现，市场对于AI 产业叙事的预期开始松动，杀估值构成行情调整
的核心驱动，此外，资金面微观结构恶化放大波动。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”
反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从
DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。

2.  分子端：增长由高波动转向温和收敛，盈利增长降速，K 型分化收敛

总量层面：2027 年美股盈利增长动能或边际放缓。结构层面：2027 年美国AI 与非AI 板块间的 K 型分化
有望收敛，周期和科技对于美股盈利增长的贡献趋于降速，盈利动能贡献有望更均衡。具体而言：（1）科技板
块：高基数效应下，若下游商业化兑现不及预期或进展偏慢，AI 算力链周期属性强化，2027 年美股科技板块
盈利增长降速是中性假设；（ 2）能源板块：地缘和油价是核心变量，高基数效应下2027 年盈利增长趋于降
速；（3）传统板块：财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年的高位回落，房地产、传统资本开支
与低收入消费修复节奏更多取决于通胀和宏观流动性。

2.1  宏观：增长由高波动转向温和收敛 
增长由高波动转向温和收敛，通胀有望趋势回落，K 型分化收敛。2027 年美国实际GDP 季环比折年率四个
季度分别预计为2.0%、2.1%、2.1%和2.2%，全年均值约2.1%，较2026 年的2.5%回落0.4 个百分点；但季
度间振幅由2026 年的3.2 个百分点降至0.2 个百分点。2026 年的高波动源于政府停摆后的基数扰动、关税与
能源价格冲击以及AI 投资的一次性脉冲，这些因素在2027 年基本淡出，增长回到2%上下的潜在增速附近。
以四季度同比衡量，2027 年预期为2.1%，较2026 年同期的2.3%小幅回落0.2 个百分点，全年同比在2.0%—
2.3%之间，呈"中枢微降、路径平坦"的形态。驱动来看，财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年
的高位回落；房地产、传统资本开支与低收入消费受高利率压制更为明显；AI 相关投资仍在贡献增量，但边际
增速回落。 
图 13：美国主要宏观经济指标展望及美联储 9 月 SEP 展望（26Q4 后为预期值）

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

2.1.1  中期选举后政经背景变化及对市场的影响 
展望前方，经济议题将是2027 年乃至2028 年大选年中最关键的政治博弈战场，“降低通胀”和“弥合经
济K 分化”将是持续性的政治诉求——分别对应着降低油价的天然诉求和对AI 板块的监管、再分配诉求。 
本次中期选举点出美国政经气候变化最大的特征，是选民焦点已经从意识形态的“左右”之争转为经济阶
层的“上下”之争、从文化之争转为经济之争，“K 型分化”最终清晰的显示在投票版图中，成为民主党的
Tailwind。在“由红转蓝”的部分关键摇摆州，民主党候选人以一种对“意识形态”分歧较为克制和中立的态
度，但火力全开直指降低物价等经济诉求。在过去数十年的选举周期中，“左右”之争中如移民问题、少数群
体、宗教、“美国至上”（MAGA）、去精英化等议题，在当前高物价、K 型分化、贫富分化等经济失衡加剧的背景
下再度煽动意识形态武器已然不合时宜，而共和党候选人在应对经济议题时显然无法与特朗普政府进行切割。
事实上，民主党正在获得部分MAGA 选票，2024 共和党的MAGA 群体与2026 民主党的劳动人民群体在经济逻辑
上并没有那么大差异。蓝色狂潮必然会阻碍科技和AI 进展？我们不这么认为，基本面逻辑而非政经变化仍旧
是我们前期相对看空科技板块的主因，选举只是阶段性强化这一方向。但是必须要看到“Technocracy”或者
“科技寡头-政治”的联盟已经成为当前经济背景下的对立面。

### Page 8

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 
 8 
图 14：2026 年美国中期选举选民议题关注度排序

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

落到对金融市场影响看，美国政经变化如何影响中短期市场，哪些领域政策变化概率较大、哪些领域实质
性影响不大？——我们从三类路径看可为和不可为。一是不依赖新立法、总统可自行决定：如232、301 关税
已在实际执行，对华301 条款10 月7 日确认继续有效；先进芯片出口许可与对外投资审查已有法律基础（《综
合对外投资国家安全法》）、军事行动属总统权限——无太大变化，国会能做的只有拨款附加条件、听证和诉
讼，属于降低行政执行效率的施压手段。二是需要新立法+参议院60 票+总统签署：如企业税率从21%上调至
25%—28%、恢复ACA 增强补贴与联邦医保补贴、恢复被削减的IRA 清洁能源税惠、收回总统关税授权等——民
主党推行自己的议程并非易事，对当前市场主线影响不大。普通法案在参议院需60 票才能结束辩论，推翻否
决需两院各三分之二多数。三是必须通过的法案与参议院专属权力：如12 月11 日到期的临时拨款（覆盖全部
12 项年度拨款）、已在众议院以216:212 通过的2027 财年国防授权法、延至12 月11 日的《国防生产法》授
权、2027 年债务上限；以及参议院人事确认权（美联储理事、联邦法官、内阁）与众议院传票、监督权等。—
—重要财政变化和显著债务扩张难度仍大，但我们会更关注年底债务上限博弈和政府停摆风险。

止共和党议程，而非推行自己的议程

2.1.2  地缘-油价-通胀情景如何演变？通胀依赖同比基数效应

我们认为2027 年美国通胀趋势有所回落、中枢下降。但通胀趋势依赖同比基数，且近端2026Q4-2027Q1
通胀读数仍旧较高，2027Q2 同比基数效应显现。 
当前中东地缘形式总体看既保留谈判通道但局势也未实质性降温。2026 年2 月底美伊冲突爆发以来，霍
尔木兹海峡通行受伊朗方面制约，成为双方博弈的核心筹码。美国中期选举和以色列大选关键转折，美国中期
选举中民主党夺回参众两院，以色列方面内塔尼亚胡阵营失去议会多数，两者强化了冲突降温预期。外交层
面，卡塔尔、巴基斯坦持续斡旋，伊朗提出以美方解除制裁、解冻约80 亿美元资产为条件、两天内重开海峡
的“两天计划”，伊朗扬称最高领袖已下令结束“非战非和”状态、愿以谈判破局。但实质进展尚未落地，伊朗
同时表示并未与美国直接谈判，11 月以来美军对伊朗仍有“小型打击”；伊朗革命卫队解除导弹射程限制、美
军向阿曼湾货船开火；海峡接连发生油轮触雷起火与爆炸事件，沙特与胡塞武装的互袭亦未停止。 
对于原油市场而言，我们的观点是“去繁存简”，中长期仍旧视作宏观风险，一方面，主要因地缘核心矛盾
未解决，使得供给持续回归具有脆弱性（伊朗仍有袭击海峡油轮的能力、胡塞武装仍有干扰沙特原油供应能
力），原油价格持续蕴含地缘溢价，低库存水平亦支持原油估值。但另一方面，中东供给流量逐步向战前水平
恢复、运费价格有所回落、下游需求对高油价负反馈、高油价高通胀下的政治压力等也真实抑制油价上行空
间。我们按照原油75-110 美元/桶宽幅区间震荡，来对2027 年通胀水平和趋势做测算。 
我们大致测算，油价每变动10 美元/桶，2027 年CPI 同比约变动0.35 个百分点。基准情形下：75 美元
对应约1.97%，85 美元对应约2.32%，95 美元对应约2.68%，100 美元对应约2.85%，110 美元对应约
3.21%。不过，此模型仅仅考虑“油价-汽油-通胀权重-通胀”的传导路径，未能考虑1）战略储备释放、燃油
税调整等政策对冲；2）成品油裂解价差大幅走阔风险带来更强的通胀传导；3）工资—价格螺旋与通胀预期；
4）下游需求负反馈程度。 
另外，归总市场一致预期、彭博经济学家预测、市场定价和BE Model 四方预测美国CPI 同比在2026Q4-
2027Q1 维持在3.0-3.8%区间，在2027Q2 回落至2.1-2.9%区间，在2027Q3-2027Q4 回落至1.9-2.6%区

2.2  科技：年内估值下杀已基本消化 AI 产业降速的预期，2027 年更多面临现实端盈利的考验

自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现，2027
年可能逐步确认）。

展望2027 年，AI 交易的风险点随时间累积：两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 海外股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导
（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技
股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。展望2027 年，两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛：（ 1）乐观情景下，AI 下游实现更广义的商业化兑现（AI Agent&物理AI）—>AI 投资链实现
闭环（估值修复，业绩增长）；（2）悲观情景下，逐渐确认“AI 一阶导”反转（Capex 下修/上游硬件价格崩塌）
——>市场担忧从AI 产业增速见顶（杀估值）—>AI 产业增长见顶（杀业绩）。 
图 18：股债定价背离定位 AI 投资周期进入苛刻的下半场：25Q4 以来海外信用市场率先定价 Capex 融资持
续性与回报约束的风险，而海外权益市场定价明显隐含非对称风险

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

2.2.1  CSP“ROIC-WACC”临界点渐近？AI 下游兑现与上游投资之间的“时差”是当前产业核心瑕疵 
北美云计算厂商ROIC-WACC 的临界点持续逼近：过去一年，北美 CSP 的ROIC-WACC 持续收敛，
Neocloud 的ROIC-WACC 几乎已全线倒挂。 
AI 上游 Capex 维持高强度&下游兑现放缓（ROIC↓）： 核心矛盾在于AI 下游需求兑现速度尚未充分匹配上
游基础设施投资扩张速度（26Q3 Anthropic ARR 放缓进一步激化矛盾）；另一方面，AI 投资下半场，前期巨
额Capex 投入对于利润的侵蚀正在逐步显性化，资产负债表对于下游需求兑现的容错率降低。 
AI 产业信用溢价走廓&宏观利率高企（WACC↑）： 一方面，信用利差走廓反映信用市场对于AI 投资风险的
重新定价：Capex 持续性与回报约束的焦虑加深&供给端恶化（科技企业大规模发债）；另一方面，地缘反复抬
高油价&通胀&海外宏观利率中枢亦间接抬升科技企业融资成本中枢。

观利率高企（WACC↑）

2.2.2  Capex 持续性的质疑是否会从预期走向现实？CSP 现金流的约束在显性化，对下游兑现容错率降低 
去年Q4 以来海外股债定价开始背离，今年9 月以来海外股债定价背离加深的底层逻辑：市场对AI 资本开
支可持续性的担忧，正在从对未来盈利与投资回报的预期博弈逐步转向对现实融资约束和资产负债表承载能力
的审视。截至2026Q3，北美 CSP 延续高强度 AI 资本开支，除微软外自由现金流均转负（需依靠外部融资维
持Capex），CSP 现金流约束正在逐步显性化，意味着后续对下游兑现容错率进一步降低。

2.2.3  Cycle 还是Super Cycle？若下游需求放缓难以证伪，AI 基建定价体系回归周期属性 
若AI 下游商业化放缓、AI 上游投资回报承压等风险持续累积且难以证伪，市场对AI 基础设施的定价逻
辑或将从超级周期（Super Cycle）逐步回归传统资本开支周期（Cycle）：2027 年AI 基建增长降速是中性假
设。历史经验，北美CSP 及费城半导体估值下修往往领先EPS 增速下修1-3 季度。 
自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现）。关
注：（1）GPU 租赁价格& HBM/DRAM 合约及现货价格；（ 2）头部LLM ARR 环比增速；（3）GPU 及服务器订单砍
单/延迟提货风险；（4）AI 融资链金融违约风险（主体评级、CDS 利差、IPO 认购倍数）；（5）Capex 预期是否
下修等维度。

2.2.4  蓝色浪潮对于 AI 产业实质性影响几何？供给端因素更多影响节奏，需求端兑现决定产业方向

蓝色浪潮一定程度强化了地方层面对AI 基础设施投资建设的政策约束。随着民主党在地方选举中取得进
展，部分州及地方政府可能加强对数据中心建设的监管，重点涉及电力消耗、环保标准、土地使用、税收优惠
及公用事业成本等方面，因此可能推高数据中心建设及运营成本，延长项目审批周期，并对新增产能的落地节
奏形成约束。 
整体而言，政策约束更多作用于AI 基础设施的供给侧，可能影响资本开支的落地节奏、建设成本及区域
布局，而AI 下游需求端兑现最终决定产业方向。一方面，相关限制措施因州而异，且多数重大政策调整仍需
经过立法、监管及行政审批程序，实际落地存在一定时滞；另一方面，已经开工或完成前期审批的项目短期受
到的影响相对有限，CSP 也可以通过调整选址、迁移项目及增加能源配套投资等方式应对。

3.  分母端：宏观流动性先紧后松，AI 信用局部风险暴露

3.1  宏观流动性：我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松 
2027 年宏观流动性对整体宏观情绪定调有重要影响，我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松。当前美国
金融条件已处于边际收紧态势，主要来自利率上行、美元指数走强、信用利差走阔和美股下行多种原因共振。 
美联储预计在12 月和明年3 月各加息25bp 至4.25%-4.50%，此后按兵不动。也有观点认为降息最早在
2027 年11 月启动。因此，全年大致是上半年维持偏紧、下半年视利率回落程度、股票市场和信用市场以及美
元指数从“危机模式”中走出，带动整体金融条件指数逐步转松。 
分项看2027 年宏观流动性： 
一方面看货币政策，上半年仍旧处在升息曲线中，对宏观流动性有所限制，三季度触峰回落对宏观流动性
边际贡献宽松，对加息结束的定价或在通胀显著回落或油价明显下行的可能性下重新开启。当前市场预计
2026 年12 月会议加息约1 次，此后加息预期继续累积，2027 年1 月底加息一次，隐含利率约4.27%；至3
月再度加息，隐含利率升至4.4%以上。加息定价在二季度明显放缓，2027 年7 月至9 月前后见顶，峰值隐含
利率约4.55%，相当于较当前再上行约65bp 左右。2027 三季度后，曲线小幅回落，至2027 年11 月至12 月
隐含利率回落至4.45%左右。这说明市场预期本轮加息在2027 年年中结束，下半年开始计入小幅降息，但全
年政策利率仍明显高于当前水平。 
另一方面看金融条件如股票市场、信用市场和美元指数，整体上看，当前我们认为股票市场调整在中期尚
未结束，而信用市场尤其是科技板块自由现金流恶化预计延续至2027 年中，信用利差或对金融条件指数构成
拖累，美元指数跟随利差逻辑和市场风偏较难判断，但预计美元指数在2027Q1 维持相对强势，对金融条件构
成抑制，而后回落对边际改善金融条件。

3.2  信用风险：AI 产业融资循环链条局部信用风险值得动态关注 
3.2.1  AI 基建投资正在明显增加产业链相关公司对债务融资的依赖 
据IMF 数据：北美云服务商在全球投资级债券发行中的份额从2024 年的不足1%升至2026 年的约10%。
虽然其核心业务盈利强劲，但持续高额的Capex 投资使得FCF 快速下降（除微软外均已转负），AI 基建投资正
在明显增加对债务融资的依赖。而Neocloud 等企业通常负债更高、盈利能力更弱，可能成为AI 生态体系中的
薄弱环节。

 22

3.2.2  AI 循环融资系统（Circular Financing）加大了债务、股权和未来收入之间的相关性

据《Urbanomics ：Update on the AI spending boom》分析：AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的
风险——循环融资（Circular Financing）、供应商融资（Vendor Financing）和表外杠杆（Off-
balance-sheet Leverage）。它们并非相互独立的融资交易，而是横跨七类产业参与者、相互关联的信用风
险结构。 
从融资机制看，AI 产业正在形成资金循环，使得原本相互独立的双边交易，演变为具有自我强化机制的融
资体系。芯片厂商及Hyperscalers 向AI Lab 提供股权投资，AI Lab 随后将获得的资金转化为对芯片厂商和
云服务商的采购承诺，这些采购承诺进一步形成供应商的在手订单（Backlog）并支撑其债券融资；与此同
时，金融机构通过SPV 和私人信贷为整个AI 基础设施建设提供资金。 
AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的风险：（1）AI 循环融资可能放大表观需求。（2）关键主体的风险可
能沿AI 产业链同步传导，原本分散在不同环节的风险实际上依赖于同一底层需求假设。如果OpenAI 无法履行
相关承诺，Oracle/CoreWeave 的在手订单、NVIDIA 与AMD 的销售预期，以及SPV 贷款人的抵押品价值，都
可能同时受到重新定价。（3）风险可能向透明度较低的融资渠道转移（私人信贷和SPV 等结构），近两年私人
信贷市场的杠杆与流动性风险持续上升。 
总体而言，只有当AI 需求不及预期时，相关风险才可能集中显现，但由于七类参与者实际上共同承担对
AI 需求增长的押注，一旦需求明显放缓，相关风险可能在多个环节同步暴露，潜在冲击可能通过企业、金融机
构及居民资产负债表向金融市场和实体经济扩散。 
图 49：AI 循环融资（Circular Financing）的七类交易主体

3.2.3  2027 年尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 链条局部信用风险值得动态关注

展望2027 年：美国尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 链条局部信用及流动性脆弱性有所累积。当
前，美国银行体系资本充足率仍接近历史较高水平，企业与居民债务占GDP 比重持续回落，整体债务脆弱性及
信用风险相对可控，尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 产业链条循环融资体系内结构性风险值得关
注。

 25 
4.  美股结论与投资展望：增配价值，回归均衡，谋定而后动

市场判断：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大
于指数整体波动，AI 产业景气度定全年增长弹性，通胀&利率定全年交易节奏。 
战略层面：增配价值与防御，回归均衡配置。我们去年以来持续提示的AI 产业四阶段框架，2027 年大致
处于第二阶段向第三阶段逐步过渡的窗口：建议降低AI 硬件，增配必选消费、医疗保健、公用事业等低AI 相
关性资产，回归盈利质量与现金流稳定性增强组合韧性。更长期的视角（第四阶段）， AI 下游（CSP/应用/软
件/物理AI 等）将成为主线资产（目前仍然处于左侧观察区间）。 
战术层面：顺势博弈通胀缓和与利率回落驱动的修复行情。若通胀改善、利率中枢下行且经济保持韧性，
金融条件改善有望催化房地产、可选消费等传统型利率敏感资产+医疗保健代表的成长型利率敏感资产的阶段性
反弹。

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：   
環境：鴻海採用 TCFD 框架為基礎，全面盤點與評估氣候相關風險與機會，
並導入 ISO 14001 環境管理系統與 ISO 45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，同時
也推動「低碳供應鏈轉型推動計畫」，協助超過 30 家供應商完成節能專案，
累計減少碳排放約 15,000 公噸 CO₂e。鴻海已有超過 150 家供應商完成碳盤
查並簽署科學基礎減碳目標（SBTi），提升能源使用效率與減碳成效。  
社會：鴻海秉持「以人為本」理念，長期支持教育與青年培訓，設立「三創
育成基金會」，提供新創團隊資金與資源支持，自 2015 年以來已經培育超
過  100 組科技創業團隊，促進創新創業生態系發展，為社會培訓科技人才。
治理：董事會有設立「永續發展委員會」，主管由具備三年以上公開發行公
司財務主管經歷之財務長兼任，該委員自 2023 年起每年召開至少四次會議，
全體委員及相關主管亦於任職期間完成 ESG 專業訓練課程，強化永續治理
能力與資訊揭露透明度。 
公司簡介：  
鴻海精密工業股份有限公司（Hon Hai Precision Industry），成立於 1974 年，
由郭台銘創辦，總部位於新北市土城區。公司早期以模具及電子零組件製造
起家，逐步擴展至電子產品組裝、系統整合及設計製造服務，現為全球最大
電子專業製造服務（EMS）廠商。 
集團業務涵蓋消費智能產品、雲端網路產品、電腦終端產品及元件與其他產
品四大領域，旗下包含工業富聯（FII）、鴻騰精密（FIT）等重要子公司，
並透過全球事業單位及子公司分工，提供零組件、模組至整機系統的垂直整
合服務。集團生產及服務據點遍及全球逾 20 個國家及地區，包括中國、台
灣、印度、越南、墨西哥及美國等，全球員工人數於季節性高峰約達 100 萬
人。 
鴻海主要競爭對手包括廣達、緯創、和碩、仁寶及立訊精密等，依產品領域
有所不同。近年積極布局 AI 伺服器、電動車及半導體等新興業務，其中 AI
伺服器受惠全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）資本支出擴張，成為重要成長動能，
並持續鞏固全球電子製造服務龍頭地位。 
3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元： 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，QoQ+19%，
YoY+46%，毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-0.1ppts，YoY-0.33ppts，主要受產品組合及
原物料上漲影響導致毛利率下滑；營業利益 1,154.99 億元，QoQ+22%，
YoY+64%，營益率 3.83%， QoQ+0.07ppts， YoY+0.4ppts，營業利益成長高
於營收及營業毛利，顯現隨營運規模擴大，生產進入規模經濟；歸屬母公司
淨利 775.24 億元，QoQ+29%， YoY+34%，稅後 EPS 5.51 元。營業利益、淨
利和 EPS 皆創下歷史單季新高。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.7%，預估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+11.7%：鴻海 10/2026 營
收 1.31 兆元，MoM+12.7%， YoY+45.7%，創單月歷史新高，除受惠 GB300
持續放量外，iPhone 新機推出亦帶動營收顯著增長；Vera Rubin 僅小量出
貨。展望 4Q26，我們預期 GB200/300 出貨量季增持平至微幅下滑，Vera 
Rubin 則有雙位數以上的成長，並且由於 Vera Rubin 的 ASP 近乎倍增，預
估伺服器營收將季增雙位數。消費電子方面，我們認為記憶體與處理器成本

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

3 群益投顧 
上升，將使手機出貨面臨壓力，但產品 ASP 提升及 Apple 穩健的品牌效應
仍將為出貨量提供支撐，因此 4Q26 仍將受惠新機推出與傳統旺季帶來的成
長。相較於消費電子的季節性，GB200/300 放量與產品組合變化對全年營運
的影響更大，因此 2026 年的主要成長引擎仍是伺服器業務的擴張；Vera 
Rubin 則為 2027 年的成長建立基礎。綜上所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 3.37
兆元，QoQ+11.7%；毛利率/營益率持續受 Vera Rubin 新平台出貨而稀釋，
下滑 0.10ppts/0.06ppts 來到 5.92%/3.77%，歸屬母公司淨利 899.02 億元，
QoQ+15.97%，稅後 EPS 6.39 元。 
雲端網路成為營運主軸，規模經濟支持本業獲利成長： 3Q26 雲端網路產品
占營收 54%，高於 2Q26 的 51%。消費智能、電腦終端及元件與其他產品分
別占 29%、12%及 5%。雲端網路產品受 AI 伺服器及機櫃出貨帶動，季增與
年增均強勁。消費智能產品亦受新品與傳統旺季帶動，季增優於公司原先預
期。據我們所知，伺服器約占雲端網路營收的 85%-90%，其中約 70%來自
AI 伺服器。隨雲端網路占比提高，AI 機櫃的交付時程、平台組合及物料認
列方式，將更直接影響鴻海的營收與獲利率。鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-
0.1ppts，主要受到整櫃 GPU 伺服器高昂的 BOM 所 稀 釋；營 業 利 益 率 3.83%，
QoQ+0.07ppts，雖然毛利率季減，營益率卻有所改善，反映出營收規模擴大
及營運效率提升的效益。 
Vera Rubin 接力 GB300，2027 年進入新平台主要貢獻期：AI 機櫃的新舊平
台交替是鴻海延續成長的核心動能。鴻海預計 2026 年 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨
較 2025 年倍增以上，且既有 GB 平台需求可能延續至 2027 年。此外，Vera 
Rubin 與 GB 系列同為 NVL72，因此我們預期鴻海將可較平滑的實現世代交
替。管理層預估 4Q26 Vera Rubin 出貨量將呈高雙位數增長。展望 2027 年，
伺服器業務上會新增更多 Neocloud 客戶，但主要客戶仍然是大型 CSP。整
體訂單並未隨近期市場的擔憂而調整，對於 AI 投資的展望維持正向，預估
AI 機櫃出貨量將呈高雙位數成長。 
自製零組件與自動化支撐獲利，高 BOM 仍主導毛利率走勢：鴻海透過垂直
整合提高單一專案可承接的製造內容，管理層表示除規模經濟外，零組件自
製率的提高與自動化生產都將為毛利率及營益率提供支撐。根據我們調查，
在晶片以外的零組件自製率約 50%，布局包括連接器、線材、Midplane、交
換器及散熱相關產品，零組件毛利率高於 15%，部分甚至可達 20%，管理層
亦提出 800G 以上交換器全年營收倍增的展望，這些產品有助鴻海由機櫃組
裝向高速互聯及關鍵元件延伸，並可提升附加價值。然而據我們了解目前鴻
海在 GPU 的大型專案中尚未採用 consignment model，記憶體等元件漲價則
主要透過 cost-plus 模式轉嫁給客戶，進入 Vera Rubin 世代後，由於 ASP 將
近倍增，會進一步造成毛利率稀釋，考慮到 GPU Rack 單價極高，我們預估
2027 年毛利率仍將承壓。 
ASIC 拓展產品組合，物料模式與 ASP 差異使營收比重低於出貨量佔比：據
我們了解，鴻海 ASIC 平台約佔 AI 伺服器營收的 20%，較 1H26 略升，且
ASIC 平台成長速度高於 GPU 平台，除既有客戶外亦有新專案，管理層預估
3Q26 ASIC 伺服器出貨量 YoY+150%，高於 GPU 伺服器 70%的出貨量年增。
我們認為 ASIC 專案增加可擴大鴻海在不同運算平台的參與，減少成長來源
集中於單一 GPU 平台的程度。此外，大部分 ASIC 專案將有望採用
consignment model，有助於緩解 AI 伺服器對於整體毛利率的負面效果，然
而 GPU 平台 ASP 顯著高於 ASIC 平台，因此 ASIC 的營收較難完整反映其

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

4 群益投顧 
專案及製造規模。我們認為目前 ASIC 專案採用 consignment model 尚不足
以顯著改善 GPU Rack 高 BOM 對整體毛利率的稀釋。 
策略投資三菱電機，電動車可望成為未來另一成長引擎：鴻海透過與三菱電
機的策略結盟深化在電動車領域的發展，雖投資金額小於 AI 相關的支出，
但將持續投入研發，目前已做好充足準備量產，將尋機大規模進入 EV 市場，
可望成為未來的另一成長動能。 
預估 2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，稅後 EPS 24.71 元：我們預估隨 AI 機櫃放量
將提高營收與獲利規模，2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，YoY+37.1%，毛利率 5.65%，
YoY-0.4ppts；營業利益 5,181.73 億元，YoY+25.5%，營益率 3.43%，YoY-
0.3ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 3,478.27 億元，YoY+25.4%，稅後 EPS 24.71 元。
Vera Rubin 由初期出貨轉為全年營收貢獻，加上既有 GB300 需求延續及
ASIC 業務擴張，帶動全年營收顯著增長。公司持續布局台灣、美國、墨西
哥及越南產能，亦有助因應客戶在不同地區的交付需求。 
投資建議：受惠 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，2026 AI 機櫃出貨可望倍數增長。
此外我們預期憑藉 iPhone 的品牌效應，4Q26 智慧型消費電子業務可望維持
過往的傳統出貨旺季。展望 2027 年，Vera Rubin 將成為主要成長引擎，主
要客戶對於 AI 伺服器的需求依舊強勁。關鍵製程自動化及零組件自製率的
提升亦有助提升營運效率，為毛利率及營益率提供部分支撐。考量上述原
因，我們給予鴻海 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
ASPEED Technology Inc
Near-term BMC momentum peaking;
downgrade to Neutral
Three reasons for our downgrade to Neutral
We remain constructive about Aspeed’s growth potential and competitive positioning
longer term, but are downgrading the stock from Buy to Neutral on limited upside vs
expectation in 2027-28. Key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied
to overall Cloud AI TAM expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US

mid-terms and constraint of power / datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that
Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market server units in 2026, leading to
uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases / demand slows; 3) we
see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
Possible order moderation in 2027, after some over-build this year
Aspeed's BMC units grew 78% YoY to reach ~34mn in 2026, of which we estimate

9.5mn units for accelerator servers, and 23.5mn units for general purpose servers. Our
analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds
the end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at
hyperscalers which could cap the upside into 2027. We note that healthy shipment ratio
of Aspeed's BMC units vs end market should be ~80-90% considering Aspeed's 80%
market share, with 5-10% upside for switch, power supply etc. The ratio likely rises to

104% in 2026 from 70%+ in 2024-25. See Figure 1-3 for our analysis.   We lower 2027
BMC units from 48.2mn to 42.4mn, assuming the BMC shipment to reach near term
peak of 11-12m/Q in Q426-Q127, and to soften moderately to ~10m/Q in H227. We
still consider robust growth of AI accelerater BMCs at 82% in 2027E vs 76% in 2026E.
Product roadmap reinforces long-term leadership
Longer term, we remain constructive on Aspeed’s strong execution, accelerated product

roadmap, and rising silicon content per server. Aspeed remains highly focused on BMC
development, unlike  many competitors where BMC is one of several priorities. Its
roadmap, including more advanced BMCs such as the 6nm AST2800, enhanced security
features, and adjacent products such as SMC, I/O expanders and FPGA integration,
should reinforce its leading position in the Cloud AI market over the next 3-5 years. See
our view on Marvell’s infrastructure management controller (IMC) introduction.

Valuation: D/G to Neutral from Buy; lower target to NT$10,000 from NT$19,000
We trim 2027/28E EPS by 14%/11%. Our new estimates are 10/12% below VA
consensus. Aspeed's order outlook remains robust for 2027, but we see risk of
moderation in coming quarters. Downgrade to Neutral from Buy. Our new target of NT
$10,000 is based on 30x 2027E PE, as we roll back from 43x 2027-28E PE given the
rising uncertainty. 30x PE is below Aspeed's historical mid-cycle of 36x, but we view

reasonable considering the potential demand slowdown and 30% LT earnings CAGR.

### Page 2

ASPEED Technology Inc UBS Research
Thesis MapUBS Research THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Can BMC units continue to grow in the next two years after rising 78% in 2026E?
Although we remain constructive about Aspeed's long term execution and technology leadership,
we see a possible order moderation into 2027 after over-build in 2026E. We estimate that BMC
shipment will rise 78% YoY in 2026E and reach around 34m total BMC shipment units with 23.5m

dedicated for general purpose servers—this suggests potential pre-building of BMC inventory by
hyperscalers, as end market general purpose server units are only at 22.7m units in 2026E. In
addition, we are also monitoring the risk of AI data center buildout slowdown in 2028 post the recent
US elections and power constraints. We now forecast BMC shipment growth to moderate in
2027E/28E, with 25%/13% YoY unit shipment growth respectively.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain its dominant 70%-plus market share in server BMCs?

Yes. We expect Aspeed to maintain its market share in the next 3-4 years due to 1) We expect
migration to AST2700 to accelerate in 2027 from a mild 2026 ramp up, with new Intel and AMD CPU
platforms as well as key cloud ASIC projects and Nvidia's new Rubin Ultra AI GPU platform in 2027; 2)
Aspeed has enhanced its BMC offering by embedding flexibility to satisfy customer requirements; 3)
Aspeed is expanding its product portfolio to I/O expanders that integrate functionalities built on

FPGA, RoT security chips, and mini-BMCs (SMCs). 4) Aggressive business strategy to build inventory
for hyperscalers since 2025, which we believe should limit opportunity for competitors.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain overall gross margins at a high-60s level?
Yes. We expect Aspeed's GM to remain in the high-60s over the next few years. Drivers include: 1) the
roll-out of the higher-end AST2700 series, which could solidify Aspeed's competitive position and

help it sustain market share; 2) Aspeed managing back-end logistics for AST2700, reducing reliance
on GUC; and 3) Aspeed raising prices (effective Dec 1) for BMCs to pass through cost inflation.
We are constructive for Aspeed's long-term competitive positioning but are downgrading it to
Neutral. Our key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied to overall Cloud AI TAM
expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US mid-terms and constraint of power /

datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market
server units in 2026, leading to uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases /
demand slows; 3) we see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
1) Our analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds the
end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at hyperscalers which

could cap the upside into 2027. 2) The outcome of recent US mid-terms creates higher uncertainty
for new AI data center build-out in 2028.
Aspeed's share price has rallied ~65% YTD (vs Taipei Exchange Index's 11% gain), but declined 54%
from its peak in Sep. The stock is now trading at 29x/22x 2027/28E PE, de-rated from its peak of
around 63x/48x in Sept, suggesting the market is taking a more conservative stance regarding overall
Cloud AI outlook and Aspeed's growth.

Company Description
ASPEED is the world's largest baseboard management controller (BMC) chipset provider.
ASPEED introduced the first generation of server management processors in 2005. It acquired
Emulex Pilot's remote server management chip business in 2016. Besides server management

chipsets, ASPEED also produces PC/AV extension solutions and image processing system-on-
chips (SoCs). In 2023, server management chipsets accounted for 88% of total sales.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We derive our price target for Aspeed from PE valuation methodology relative to its historical
trading range.
We believe key upside risks for Aspeed include: 1) stronger-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers and from AI servers with less crowding out of traditional

servers; 2) further market share expansion; 3) faster-than-expected server platform upgrades;
4) faster-than-expected progress in expanding the addressable content within servers.
We believe key downside risks for Aspeed include: 1) weaker-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers; 2) market share losses or more intense industry competition; 3)
slower-than-expected server platform upgrades; 4) slower-than-expected progress in
expanding the addressable content within servers.

Quantitative Research Review
UBS Global Research publishes a quantitative assessment of its analysts' responses to certain questions about the likelihood of an occurrence
of a number of short term factors in a product known as the 'Quantitative Research Review'.  The views for this month can be found below.
Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.

by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

新代(7750)：中國高階控制器需求持續強勁 
建議：評等買進

一、新代為台灣精密設備控制器
大廠 
 新代成立於 1995 年，為專注於精密設備控制器之大廠，以自有品牌 SYNTEC
行銷全球。主營產品涵蓋 CNC 數控系統、伺服產品與產業專機；集團亦積極佈
局新成長動能，旗下「聯達智能」與「正鉑雷射」分別專攻機器人/雲端智造
（IoT/MES）及工業減碳製程設備。公司具備兩大核心優勢：其一為精準的市場
定位，產品品質媲美德日大廠（如 Siemens、Fanuc），同時具備極佳的性價比，
成功填補高階進口與中低階國產間的市場缺口；其二為軟硬整合與在地服務，透
過橫跨歐美、亞太及中國 24 個分公司的全球據點，提供即時服務以協助機械廠
進行差異化升級。目前新代服務逾 2,000 家客戶，單一客戶營收占比均低於 5%，
客戶結構極具分散性與營運韌性，築起穩固的長期競爭護城河。

二、產品組合優化抵銷原物料成
本上漲，3Q26 毛利維持約 44%
水準 
 受惠中國工具機及金屬加工產業需求強勁、終端客戶持續升級高階控制器，以及
競爭對手關鍵零組件供應受限帶動轉單，新代 3Q26 營收達 78.05 億元，QoQ-
5.5%、YoY+157.7%，雖受傳統淡季影響較上季下滑，但仍創歷史單季次高。獲
利方面， 儘管記憶體價格上漲，受惠高階控制器銷售比重提升及產品組合優化，
3Q26 毛利率達 44.44%，季增 0.35 個百分點、年減 0.04 個百分點，維持公司
長期指引 45%±2%區間； 隨營收規模擴大及費用率改善，3Q26 營業利益達
21.06 億元，QoQ-11.9%、YoY+398.2%，營業利益率達 26.98%，年增 13.02
個百分點；稅後淨利達 16.80 億元，QoQ-11.4%、YoY+360.2%，單季 EPS 達
24.06 元，創歷史單季次高。1-3Q26 稅後淨利達 46.50 億元，YoY+221.7%，
EPS 達 66.59 元，反映高階產品需求成長、轉單效益及營運規模擴張持續挹注
獲利。

三、中國高階控制器滲透率持續
提升，海外布局與產能擴張挹注
2027 年成長動能 
 2026 年中國工具機產業維持成長動能，1–8 月金屬切削相關產業成長約 18–
19%，受惠製造業升級、五軸加工及車銑複合機等高階設備需求增加，帶動控制
器與伺服系統銷售。新代憑藉高階產品性能、較國際競品約 20%的價格優勢，
以及中國逾 2,000 家活躍客戶與在地直銷服務網絡，持續擴大市占率，2026 年
控制器出貨量預估 YoY+70–80%，並受惠日系競爭對手關鍵零組件供應受限，
取得大型工具機客戶轉單。儘管部分日系廠商供貨逐步恢復，公司透過提前備貨
及與記憶體原廠簽訂供應協議，預期關鍵零組件供應可維持至 2027 年下半年，
搭配產品組合優化及客戶黏著度提升，有望延續競爭優勢。海外布局方面，印度
市場受惠製造業在地化政策及工具機產業升級需求，帶動高階控制器國產替代
與供應商多元化商機。公司已與印度前十大工具機廠建立合作接洽，並取得兩筆
單筆超過 100 套的訂單，預期 2026 年印度營收 YoY+100%，惟目前營收貢獻

仍低；中長期目標五年內印度市占率提升至 10%，並持續擴大東南亞市場布局。
產能方面，蘇州二廠預計於 2027 年農曆年前後完成搬遷，馬來西亞新廠預計
1Q27 完工，隨新產能陸續投入，預計 1H27 整體產能將擴充至目前約 2.6 倍，
另有機械手臂產能增加，為後續營收成長提供支撐。展望 2027 年，管理層預期
中國工具機產業成長約 10–15%，公司目標營收成長 25–30%，並持續推動新代
控制器、聯達機械手臂及正鉑智慧工廠整合方案，搭配海外市場拓展及 AI 技術
導入，帶動營收與獲利持續成長。

四、評等買進理由  綜合前述，展望 2027 年，新代受惠中國高階工具機需求成長、日系競爭對手轉
單效益延續，以及控制器市占率持續提升，營運成長動能可望延續。隨蘇州二廠
及馬來西亞新廠陸續投產，預計自 2Q27 起新增產能逐步開出，整體控制器產能
有望擴充至目前約 2.5 倍，搭配印度大型工具機客戶導入加速，以及多合一高階
控制器與智慧自動化產品持續推出，有助推升產品 ASP 及獲利能力，預估 2026
年稅 EPS 為 91.49，預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 為 118.49。至於評價面部分，過往
3 年 PER 平均落在 25x-35x，目前約 16x，處歷史區間下緣，中國市占率提升、
印度市場拓展、新產能貢獻及智慧製造業務成長等中長期動能，認為目前評價具
吸引力，故給予買進評等，目標價 2300 元(25x2026EPS)。

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적

Document 253: A primer on fiscal measures debated in French elections_ Most candidates commit to restoring fiscal sustainability, with uneven credibility (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/A primer on fiscal measures debated in French elections_ Most candidates commit to restoring fiscal sustainability, with uneven credibility (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026FOCUS — FRENCH ELECTIONS: A PRIMER ON FISCAL MEASURES
A PRIMER ON FISCAL MEASURES DEBATED IN FRENCH ELECTIONS: MOST CANDIDATES COMMIT TO RESTORING FISCAL
SUSTAINABILITY, WITH UNEVEN CREDIBILITY
1 Other growth-critical policy proposals will be reviewed in thematic deep-dives to be published over the coming months.
This note offers an overview of the measures proposed by presidential election candidates from the main political groups,

namely Marine Le Pen, Jean-Luc Mélenchon, Édouard Philippe, Raphaël Glucksmann, David Lisnard and Bruno Retailleau.
The focus here is on their proposals for public finances and pensions1. Overall, budget plans go in the right direction but
rely on overly optimistic costs and revenue assumptions. However, in several cases a proposed “golden rule” enhances
credibility. The proposals regarding pensions improve on the status quo in most cases, including, in some cases, by intro-

ducing a capitalisation pillar. However, most safeguard costly features of the current system, without enough measures to
offset their impact fully.
KEY TAKEAWAYS ON PUBLIC FINANCES (TABLE 1)
- The bulk of the candidates target a below 3% of GDP fiscal deficit by 2032. The budget proposals are often optimistic (or lack sufficient detail),
which is a common feature of previous presidential campaigns (e.g., in 2017, E. Macron proposed reducing public spending to 50% of GDP by

2022).
- A majority of candidates propose a “golden rule”, including the National Rally (Rassemblement national). A rule would lend credibility to their
fiscal path (if the planned measures are insufficient, the golden rule would require them to adopt additional measures, as it was done e.g. in
Germany under the debt brake).
- The budget announcements made by several candidates — including the National Rally (RN) and La France Insoumise (LFI) — are expected

to worsen the fiscal deficit in 2027. According to our calculations, a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in a 2 pp increase in the
public debt-to-GDP ratio by the end of that consolidation (in 2032, debt would reach 126.5% of GDP instead of 124.5% without this delay), and
even more if the deficit or interest rate conditions were to deteriorate significantly.
- In the absence of a golden rule, LFI’s commitment to fiscal consolidation remains uncertain. The candidate’s promises are very costly, leading

to a clear increase in the fiscal deficit. Some of the proposed measures are confiscatory in nature and would likely shrink the tax base. The can-
didate proposes no binding safeguards to ensure that the deficit does not spiral out of control. Furthermore, his proposal to write off part of the
public debt held by the ECB could be interpreted as a way to avoid having to undertake fiscal consolidation efforts to stabilise public debt (not
to mention its feasibility).

KEY TAKEAWAYS ON PENSIONS  (TABLE 2)
- The candidates’ positions on pensions reflect real, underlying progress, particularly in order to mitigate the impact of the repeal of the Borne
reform (retirement age back to 62 years old) through savings on other parameters (contribution period, or tightened conditions such as an in-
creased discount per missing quarter).
- The prospect of fully indexing pensions, as proposed in several candidates' platforms, would be very costly (nearly 2% of GDP).

- Lowering the retirement age and full indexation undermine the system’s long-term financial stability. Everything else equal, they also reduce
the size of the active labour force, a headwind to growth and fiscal sustainability.
- Édouard Philippe, David Lisnard and Bruno Retailleau advocate raising the retirement age to 65, and Édouard Philippe also calls for extending
the contribution period to 45 years (while maintaining more favourable treatment for long careers and physically demanding jobs). The savings

associated with extending working careers represent a significant contribution to the announced fiscal consolidation effort.
- With regard to capitalisation, there are marked differences between the candidates who currently rule it out (J.L. Mélenchon, R. Glucksmann)
and the others, who have all made proposals along these lines (including the RN).
Completed on 23 November 2026

Document 330: FX Daily_ Dollar may be heading into overshoot territory

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Dollar may be heading into overshoot territory.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

It is hard to know what to make of Monday's surprise
dollar strength. Blaming fresh unhedged inﬂows into the US
tech sector following White House intervention on Nvidia is not
that appealing as a rationale. But it may be the case. We think
the dollar is starting to head into overshoot territory and would
be careful about expecting more strength just yet

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
USD: Strange price action on Monday
It was hard to keep up with FX market price action on Monday. The
dollar seemed bid most of the day, but saw a strong leg higher
when it was announced that Jensen Huang was rejoining Nvidia
and the Nasdaq surged. One could ask: if the dollar did not fall
during last week's tech sell-oﬀ, why should it rally now? It is hard to
know the driving forces behind short-term dollar moves, but the
dollar has certainly overshot short-term rate diﬀerentials.
In recent weeks, we ﬂoated a DXY target of 104.60, which has just
about been hit. Given the prospect of a hot October core PCE print

### Page 2

on Wednesday, and now perhaps a better-supported tech
environment, it is certainly hard to ﬁght dollar strength this week.
Yet the dollar has come a long way in a short space of time and
some early month-end ﬂows in thinning holiday markets might be
responsible for Monday's outsized dollar gains.
We still see 104.60 as an important resistance area for the DXY and
would be surprised if gains can be sustained above it this week. A
more sustainable pullback in the dollar, however, may have to wait

until the Fed meeting on 9 December or the November CPI data on
10 December, where base eﬀects could start to see year-on-year
headline CPI fall sharply.
On the US calendar today is September house price data and
November consumer conﬁdence. Consumer conﬁdence is expected
to edge higher despite this month's equity correction.
Chris Turner
EUR: It's a dollar move
EUR/USD ﬁnally suﬀered some heavy losses on Monday as the
dollar turned bid across the board. As above, we are not sure an

immediate break below technical support at 1.0880 can be
sustained over a multi-day basis. But holiday-thinned markets
warn against trying to ﬁght a powerful bear trend. European
exporters will certainly appreciate softer EUR/USD levels, while the
3% year-on-year fall in the trade-weighted euro probably will not
worry the ECB excessively from a monetary policy perspective.
Expect European data to be a sideshow today, but the German Ifo
for November should be encouraging after the slight upside

surprise in the German ﬂash PMI yesterday.
Chris Turner
CEE: FX needs more hawkishness to
revive support
Monday brought a signiﬁcant revival in risk appetite, spilling over
from the global narrative into the CEE region. Despite a stronger US

dollar, the region saw rallies in both rates and FX simultaneously.
Rates in Poland and the Czech Republic have approached their local
lows. The market is currently pricing in roughly two rate hikes for
both the NBP and the CNB. Given inﬂation risks and central bank
rhetoric, this appears to be near the ﬂoor. We expect a rebound,
particularly due to rising inﬂation in the coming months.
In this context, CZK rates look more attractive, given the CNB’s

hawkish bias, with two rate hikes already delivered, compared to
the NBP’s dovish stance. We believe that if January inﬂation
surprises on the upside, the CNB could deliver further tightening.
Today, we will hear from several CNB speakers, who may provide
additional forward guidance.
FX markets saw some relief, despite the stronger US dollar.
Nevertheless, unless we see a repricing of rates toward higher
levels, rate diﬀerentials do not point to sustainable gains for

regional currencies. FX markets must rely on central banks for
support, especially while the global backdrop remains negative,
even during occasional bouts of relief like yesterday. Consequently,
we are far from fading the current weakness.
Frantisek Taborsky
HUF: The NBH will not cut rates at any
cost
Last week, the NBH signalled a dovish stance at its November
meeting. The Council discussed a rate cut, with some members
advocating for monetary easing. While the minutes, to be

published soon, should reveal the voting split and details of the
discussion, the market reaction last week demonstrated that
investors did not anticipate a rate cut this year. EUR/HUF touched
374, its highest level since the general election in April. However,
yesterday saw a shift in the NBH’s rhetoric.
In an interview, Deputy Governor Zoltan Kurali adopted a more
cautious tone. It appears the central bank was testing the waters
for a rate cut last week, but the market response suggested that the

NBH should wait longer. Ultimately, however, the outcomes of the

and nor is it investment, legal or tax advice or an oﬀer or solicitation to
purchase or sell any ﬁnancial instrument.

Download
NBH’s upcoming meetings in December and February will likely be
driven by global developments. Locally, the situation is evolving
favourably: inﬂation is in line with the NBH’s forecasts and remains

the lowest in the CEE region. Furthermore, the government has
unveiled a ﬁscal consolidation plan for the coming years, and
Moody’s has just upgraded the outlook for the sovereign rating.
We do not expect a rate cut in December. Rather, we anticipate the
ﬁrst easing to occur only in the second half of next year, given the
rising inﬂation expected in the coming months. That said, if the
global backdrop improves, the NBH could still revisit the discussion

on rate cuts as early as its December meeting. For now, however,
the key takeaway is that the NBH is mindful of the FX situation and
is not pursuing rate cuts at any cost. This should support a recovery
in the HUF, especially if the global environment becomes more
favourable, as we saw yesterday. Consequently, EUR/HUF likely has
the potential to gradually return to lower levels, with the 370 mark
potentially serving as a cap once again.
Frantisek Taborsky

Document 333: Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 20
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE). 
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 3

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

### Page 4

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% 
and the top 3 stocks (the ‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. 
The recent sell-off in chip names, reflecting fears of a slower roll-out of data centres, has 
pushed valuations down sharply, creating good upside convexity — although the range 
of outcomes is wide and highly dependent on the pace of AI capex growth. See from our 
Equity analysts: At the center of tensions around the AI datacenter build: Searching for value 
following the sector-wide pullback. 
3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

### Page 5

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

### Page 8

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

Exhibit 9: We focus on three areas: Growth, selective Defensives and beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields

Europe offers a region where lower oil prices would provide a particularly strong support

Investment focus: Selective Defensives  
The economic outlook remains resilient, but risks are skewed to the downside. Slower 
EPS growth, tighter financial conditions, elevated borrowing costs and the risk of an 
earlier turn in the AI cycle argue for greater portfolio resilience. Bond yields should 
decline modestly, but credit spreads could widen as growth slows. However, Cyclicals 
have already underperformed, while many traditional Defensives no longer look cheap

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural 
growth, strong balance sheets and shareholder returns. Areas of focus include: US—
Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.
12 November 2026   17
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

Document 377: Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

#wages #inflation #labour
Q3 Wage Price Index: award wage boost
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
16:06 November 18 2026 Share
The minimum wage and award decision lifted wages growth, but unevenly – enterprise
arrangements responded more than individual arrangements, consistent with a softer
labour market. Q3 2026: 1.1%qtr, 3.5%yr.
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 1.1% in Q3 2026, slightly above Westpac’s forecast and the
market consensus for a 1.0% gain. That increased the annual growth rate to 3.5%yr – stronger than

### Page 2

Wages by Bargaining Stream
the past six months, but a similar pace to 2025.
Today’s data also showcased the impact of the 2026 Fair Work Commission’s Annual Wage Review,
which handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award wages. This lifted wages in
aggregate, but the gains were uneven.
A significant share of the increase was driven by health care and social assistance, reflecting pay
rises for state government workers covered by enterprise agreements.

In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3. Awards and enterprise arrangements accounted for 64%
of the lift, while individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share for Q3 since 2019.
Enterprise arrangements, which are often directly linked to award outcomes, were more responsive,
while individual arrangements were less so compared to previous September quarters. That is
consistent with a softer labour market and recent business survey evidence that suggests the rise in

private sector labour costs following the award decision quickly normalised.
Overall, we see the Q3 result as a step up in the level of wages from the award decision rather than
evidence of building underlying wage pressure but we will be watching whether the strength in
enterprise bargaining persists.

### Page 3

Westpac has long believed focusing on wage inflation by wage bargaining stream can help to
understand how wages respond to the economic cycle. Australia’s labour market is split into three
categories of pay setting methods:
Australian wage adjustments, including the Fair Work Commission’s (FWC) minimum wage and award
rate decisions, tend to occur at the start of the new financial year in July. The FWC handed down a
4.75% increase for 2026, larger than the decisions from 2025 (3.50%) and 2024 (3.75%), but lower

than in 2023 (5.75%).
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3 2026. Of that increase, 0.50ppts were attributed to awards,
which was broadly in line with our expectations. However, both individual and enterprise arrangements
surprised us to the upside, contributing 0.64ppts and 0.65ppts to the quarterly increase in wages
respectively.
National minimum wage and award rates, regulated by the Fair Work Ombudsman,
Enterprise agreements, or
Individual arrangements.

### Page 4

Compared to the same quarter in previous years, each stream's contribution was the strongest in
absolute terms since 2023, largely because the overall lift was bigger than in 2024 and 2025. After
accounting for the size of the increase, enterprise arrangements were responsible for 64% of the lift,
whereas individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share since 2019.
While the larger award wage decision pushed up the overall increase, it did not flow through evenly.

Many enterprise agreements are directly linked to award outcomes, either through explicit reference to
award rates or because the award decision sets a benchmark in bargaining, which helps explain why
this stream was more responsive. By contrast, wages set under individual arrangements can be more
discretionary and depend more on employers' circumstances, so they were less responsive than in
previous years. That may reflect a softer labour market, where weaker demand for workers reduces the

pressure on employers to lift pay.
Wages by Sector
By sector, private wages (excl. bonuses) rose 1.0% in Q3, pulling up the annual pace from 3.1%yr to
3.4%yr. This marks the strongest pace of wages growth in the private sector since 2023, at the time of
the Fair Work Commission’s decision to raise award wages by a much larger 5.75%.
Private sector pay is set through a mix of arrangements, including a significant share of individual
arrangements (as discussed above). Today's result suggests this year's award wage decision may

have had some flow-on effect to the broader private sector, though, as noted, the pass-through was
uneven and appears to have been stronger for enterprise arrangements than for individual
arrangements.
There was also an element of both breadth and scale when it comes to private sector wage gains. Half
of all private sector jobs recorded a wage increase in Q3, the largest share since this series began in
2016; and of those jobs that recorded an increase, average hourly wages rose 4.5%, the strongest

since Q3 2023 (5.8%).
Public wages continue to outpace the private sector, this time with a 1.3% lift in Q3, bringing the annual
growth pace to 3.9%yr, the strongest since Q4 2025. As expected, the bulk of the gain was driven by
pay rises under enterprise agreements at the state government level, particularly for front-line workers.
Additionally, given scheduled rises under existing APS-wide enterprise agreements for Commonwealth
employees had already been delivered in previous quarters, the Commonwealth level contribution to

public sector wages growth during Q3 was modest.
Relative to the private sector, the driver of public sector wage gains looks to be more about scale than
breadth – across the 38% of public sector jobs recording a wage increase during the quarter the
average hourly wage change was 4.0%, slightly under the record high of a 4.3% recorded back in Q4
2023.

### Page 5

Summary
The Q3 result shows that the larger minimum wage and award rate decision boosted wages but the
detail suggests the lift was driven more by workers with stronger bargaining power. Awards and
enterprise arrangements, where pay outcomes are more directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s
decision or to collective bargaining, whereas individual arrangements, which are more exposed to
labour market conditions, contributed the smallest share since 2019.

That is consistent with a softer labour market – the unemployment rate has risen ½ppt since the start of
the year, and we are expecting it to peak at 5.2% mid next year. The NAB business survey points the
same way, with the increase in labour costs reported by private sector firms following the award
decision quickly normalising. This suggests the Q3 result is better read as a temporary step up in the
level of wages from the award wage decision than as evidence of building underlying wage pressure,

though we will be watching whether the strength in enterprise bargaining persists.

Document 409: Upgrading equities, downgrading gold

WS Global CIO Office 
27 November 2026

Upgrading equities,

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 27 November 2026

Charts of the week: Downbeat investor positioning, sentiment

Investor positioning and sentiment indicators suggest limited further downside in US equities  
US equity combined investor positioning trend and S&P500 3m forward returns Our Fear and Greed index

Source: Vanda, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

Editorial 
Upgrading equities, downgrading gold  
Strategy summary: US equity volatility has returned to levels 
previously seen during last year’s Liberation Day tariffs. Three 
factors drove the spike: uncertainty over AI spending after the 
Democrats’ landslide mid-term victory, Nvidia CEO Jensen 
Huang’s abrupt departure and reinstatement less than two 
weeks later, and concern that reaccelerating inflation could 
prompt a more aggressive Fed response. 
The sharp downturn in US equities from their October record 
high has taken valuations back to 2022 bear market levels. Our 
quantitative models still signal caution, but investor positioning 
and sentiment suggest the market is oversold. Having stayed 
Neutral through the sell-off, we are raising global equities to 
Overweight as valuations improve. President Trump’s 
intervention at Nvidia reinforces our view that AI spending 
remains central to the US growth outlook. We are also cutting 
gold to a Neutral allocation in our balanced foundation portfolio. 
Upgrading global equities to Overweight: We cut global 
equities to Neutral in our foundation asset allocation in late 
October, because market breadth was narrowing and we 
expected volatility around the US mid-terms. That move helped 
cushion the portfolio against the subsequent sell-off. Despite 
the Democrats’ landslide, we still expect AI spending to grow, 
though more slowly than over the past three years. Our forecast 
for AI capex remains below consensus, but we think the sell-off 
prices in a sharper spending cut than is warranted (page 4). 
Criticality of AI capex to US growth. President Trump’s 
intervention in Nvidia’s leadership underscores the importance 
of AI investment to US growth and competitiveness with China. 
S&P500 valuations are now 11% below their 10-year average, 
2027 earnings growth outlook remains resilient, while sentiment 
and positioning suggest strong 3-month forward returns (see 
chart). That combination supports a return to Overweight in 
global equities. We remain Overweight US and Asia ex-Japan 
equities, which we see as the main beneficiaries of AI spending. 
Near-term risk from Congress in transition, divided Fed: 
Volatility is likely to stay elevated as control of Congress shifts 
from Republicans to Democrats. The next flashpoint is 11 
December, when government funding expires. Defence 
spending and the Iran conflict are the main sticking points. We 
expect a continuing resolution to keep the government funded 
temporarily, leaving longer-term decisions until after 3 January, 
when Democrats take control of both chambers. 
Tariffs add uncertainty. The latest US tariff proposal against 
trade partners, if it survives a potential challenge by the new 
Democrat-led Congress, will lift the average tariff rate by 2.5ppt 
to c. 14%. That will take the rate back to February levels when 
the Supreme Court struck down prior tariffs, but well below the 
2025 shock. The tariff rates align with what has been agreed in 
trade deals, but they are likely to marginally fuel US inflation. 
Disinflation, equity volatility to challenge Fed hawks. We 
expect the Fed to raise rates for the second time this cycle on 
9 December after stronger-than-expected inflation and jobs 
reports, then hike once more in Q1 2027 as near-term inflation 
remains elevated. However, US crude oil and gasoline prices 
have fallen 20% from their October highs, and oil supply could 
exceed demand in Q1 as Middle East output returns to pre-
conflict levels. We expect inflation to ease by mid-2027. Along 
with tighter financial conditions following the equity sell-off, that 
could make Fed hawks less inclined to tighten and pave the 
way for rate cut in the second half of 2027. Equities should 
benefit as markets price out further tightening. 
Downgrading gold to Neutral: Alongside the equity upgrade, 
we are cutting gold from Overweight to Neutral, reducing its 
weight in our balanced allocation from 6% to 5%. Last week, 
we lowered our three-month and 12-month gold price forecasts 
to USD 4,000/oz and USD 4,500/oz, respectively. A resilient 
dollar, expectations of higher Fed rates and liquidity needs amid 
the equity selloff have weighed on gold this year (see page 4). 
Opportunity for those under-invested in gold. Gold’s long-
term case remains intact. Fiscal concerns and geopolitical risks 
are likely to sustain Emerging Market (EM) central bank buying. 
Gold is likely to remain volatile as markets reassess the Fed 
outlook. Still, investors with a long-term horizon who are below 
their target weight can use the recent sell-off to average in.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 27 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the drivers behind your decision to upgrade 
equities to Overweight and downgrade gold to Neutral? 
Our view: We upgrade global equities to Overweight from a 
Neutral allocation. The risks now appear priced in, while 
earnings growth remains robust for a market in a mid-cycle 
phase. This improved risk-reward strengthens our relative 
preference for equities, and despite near-term volatility, 
underpins our decision to downgrade gold to a Neutral 
allocation. While we remain constructive on the precious 
metal's recovery towards USD 4,000/oz, our latest stance on it 
reflects a more measured allocation. 
Rationale: We downgraded global equities to a Neutral allocation in 
our Global Market Outlook published on 30 October 2026. At the 
time, we expected higher near-term volatility in an equity market 
transition to a mid-cycle bull market. However, the recent sell-off 
has gone further than we expected, with much of the risks now 
priced in. The sell-off has been centred on the AI theme. While we 
acknowledge downside risk to AI capex estimates, this risk is more 
than priced in by markets, in our view.  
Global equity valuations have fallen to a 14.6x 12-month forward 
price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio, a level last seen in the 2022 bear 
market. The consensus 2027 earnings growth estimate remains 
robust at 16%, which we expect to largely materialise. We favour 
broad diversification across sectors, with an Overweight stance on 
the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, Europe ex-
UK financial and Japan financial sectors. Regionally, we remain 
Overweight US and Asia ex-Japan equities, which are expected 
to offer the highest earnings growth in 2027. 
In contrast, near-term headwinds drive our decision to downgrade 
gold to Neutral, as the metal has faced pressure from a resilient 
USD, hawkish Fed expectations and liquidation to meet margin calls 
during the equity drawdown. However, fiscal concerns and continued 
demand from EM central banks support its longer-term appeal.  
Substantial Fed tightening is already priced into rates markets. 
Moderating inflation, helped by sustained lower oil prices, could 
allow the central bank to signal an earlier or lower rate peak than 
markets expect. This would ease upward pressure on US bond 
yields and soften the USD, supporting gold's recovery.  
In the near term, strong US data could sustain USD strength and 
prolong gold's volatility. We see firm support around USD 
3,300/oz, while a sustained break above USD 3,650/oz would 
strengthen our conviction in a recovery towards USD 4,000/oz. Our 
Neutral stance on gold, therefore, balances its recovery potential 
with near-term volatility and stronger expected returns from equities. 
We retain gold as a Neutral allocation, with a 5% weight in our 
balanced portfolio, maintaining exposure to its recovery while 
favouring equities on a relative basis. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

Global equity valuations reflect a more attractive 
risk-reward following the recent sell-off
